\texttt{truthValue(}\Psi_{2}\texttt{,}f\texttt{)}\in M\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\omega^{3}_{M}(\Psi_{1})=b\\
\omega^{3}_{M}(\Psi_{2})=f\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\omega^{3}_{M}(\Psi_{1}\lor\Psi_{2})=b

Analogous for the case where
\omega^{3}_{M}(\Psi_{1})=f
and
\omega^{3}_{M}(\Psi_{2})=b
.

or

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\texttt{truthValue(}\Psi_{1}\texttt{,}b\texttt{)}\in M\\
\texttt{truthValue(}\Psi_{2}\texttt{,}b\texttt{)}\in M\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\omega^{3}_{M}(\Psi_{1})=b\\
\omega^{3}_{M}(\Psi_{2})=b\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\omega^{3}_{M}(\Psi_{1}\lor\Psi_{2})=b

(c)

\theta=f
:

•

“
\Rightarrow
”:

\displaystyle\omega^{3}_{M}(\Psi_{1}\lor\Psi_{2})=\theta

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\theta=f}{}\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\omega^{3}_{M}(\Psi_{1})=f\\
\omega^{3}_{M}(\Psi_{2})=f\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{I.H.}}{}\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\texttt{truthValue(}\Psi_{1}\texttt{,}f\texttt{)}\in M\\
\texttt{truthValue(}\Psi_{2}\texttt{,}f\texttt{)}\in M\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{(AC12)}}{}\texttt{truthValue(}\Psi_{1}\lor\Psi_{2}\texttt{,}f\texttt{)}\in M

•

“
\Leftarrow
”:

\displaystyle\texttt{truthValue(}\Psi_{1}\lor\Psi_{2}\texttt{,}f\texttt{)}\in M

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\texttt{truthValue(}\Psi_{1}\texttt{,}f\texttt{)}\in M\\
\texttt{truthValue(}\Psi_{2}\texttt{,}f\texttt{)}\in M\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\omega^{3}_{M}(\Psi_{1})=f\\
\omega^{3}_{M}(\Psi_{2})=f\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\omega^{3}_{M}(\Psi_{1}\lor\Psi_{2})=f

4.

Let
\Phi=\lnot\Psi
.

(a)

\theta=t
:

•

“
\Rightarrow
”:

\displaystyle\omega^{3}_{M}(\lnot\Psi)=\theta

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\theta=t}{}\omega^{3}_{M}(\lnot\Psi)=t

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\omega^{3}_{M}(\Psi)=f

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{I.H.}}{}\texttt{truthValue(}\Psi\texttt{,}f\texttt{)}\in M

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{(\text{AC15})}\texttt{truthValue(}\lnot\Psi\texttt{,}t\texttt{)}\in M

•

“
\Leftarrow
”:

\displaystyle\texttt{truthValue(}\lnot\Psi\texttt{,}t\texttt{)}\in M

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\texttt{truthValue(}\Psi\texttt{,}f\texttt{)}\in M

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\omega^{3}_{M}(\Psi)=f

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\omega^{3}_{M}(\lnot\Psi)=t

(b)

\theta=b
:

•

“
\Rightarrow
”:

\displaystyle\omega^{3}_{M}(\lnot\Psi)=\theta

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\theta=b}{}\omega^{3}_{M}(\lnot\Psi)=b

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\omega^{3}_{M}(\Psi)=b

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{I.H.}}{}\texttt{truthValue(}\Psi\texttt{,}b\texttt{)}\in M

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{(AC17)}}\texttt{truthValue(}\lnot\Psi\texttt{,}b\texttt{)}\in M

•

“
\Leftarrow
”:

\displaystyle\texttt{truthValue(}\lnot\Psi\texttt{,}b\texttt{)}\in M

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\texttt{truthValue(}\Psi\texttt{,}b\texttt{)}\in M

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\omega^{3}_{M}(\Psi)=b

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\omega^{3}_{M}(\lnot\Psi)=b

(c)

\theta=f
:

•

“
\Rightarrow
”:

\displaystyle\omega^{3}_{M}(\lnot\Psi)=\theta

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\theta=f}{}\Rightarrow\omega^{3}_{M}(\lnot\Psi)=f

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\omega^{3}_{M}(\Psi)=t

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{I.H.}}{}\texttt{truthValue(}\Psi\texttt{,}t\texttt{)}\in M

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{(AC16)}}\texttt{truthValue(}\lnot\Psi\texttt{,}f\texttt{)}\in M

•

“
\Leftarrow
”:

\displaystyle\texttt{truthValue(}\lnot\Psi\texttt{,}f\texttt{)}\in M

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\texttt{truthValue(}\Psi\texttt{,}t\texttt{)}\in M

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\omega^{3}_{M}(\Psi)=t

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\omega^{3}_{M}(\lnot\Psi)=f

It has been shown that
\omega^{3}_{M}(\Phi)=\theta
if and only if
\texttt{truthValue(}\Phi\texttt{,}\theta\texttt{)}\in M
.
The integrity constraint (AC23) ensures that
\texttt{truthValue(}A\texttt{,}f\texttt{)}
is never true wrt. each formula
A\in\mathcal{K}
.
In other words,
\texttt{truthValue(}A\texttt{,}f\texttt{)}\not\in M
for all
A\in\mathcal{K}
.
Consequently,
\omega^{3}_{M}(A)\neq f
for all
A\in\mathcal{K}
.
Thus,
\omega^{3}_{M}(A)
evaluates to either
t
or
b
in all cases.
This corresponds to the definition of a model in Priest’s three-valued logic.
Hence,
\omega^{3}_{M}\in\mathsf{Models}(\mathcal{K})
.
∎

Lemma 10
.

If
\omega^{3}\in\mathsf{Models}(\mathcal{K})
then
P_{\mathrm{c}}^{\prime}(\mathcal{K})
has an answer set
M
such that
\omega^{3}_{M}=\omega^{3}
.

Proof.

Analogous to the proof of Lemma
9
in reverse.
∎

Theorem
7
.

Let
M_{o}
be an optimal answer set of
P_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathcal{K})
.
Then
|(\omega^{3}_{M_{o}})^{-1}(b)|=\mathcal{I}{{}_{\mathrm{c}}}(\mathcal{K})
.

Proof.

From Lemma
9
and Lemma
10
it follows that the answer sets of
P_{\mathrm{c}}^{\prime}(\mathcal{K})
correspond exactly to the 3-valued models of
\mathcal{K}
.
The minimize statement included in
P_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathcal{K})
(AC20) then ensures that only a minimal number of instances
\texttt{truthValue(}X\texttt{,}b\texttt{)}
are included in the answer set.
This corresponds to the minimal number of atoms
X\in\mathsf{At}(\mathcal{K})
being evaluated to
b
which is exactly the definition of
\mathcal{I}{{}_{\mathrm{c}}}(\mathcal{K})
.
Note that, following (AC2), for all
X\in\mathsf{At}(\mathcal{K})
,
\texttt{atom(}X\texttt{)}
must be included in any answer set of
P_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathcal{K})
.
∎

A.2.2
The Forgetting-Based Inconsistency Measure

Encoding 8:
Overview of the ASP encoding
P_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathcal{K})
of the forgetting-based inconsistency measure
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{f}}
.

\mathcal{K}
is the given knowledge base.

For every
A\in\mathcal{K}
:

\texttt{kbMember(}A\texttt{).}

(AF1)

For every formula
\Phi_{a}
which consists of a single atom occurrence
X^{l}
:

\texttt{formulaIsAtomOcc(}\Phi\texttt{,}X\texttt{,}l\texttt{).}

(AF2)

For every conjunction
\Phi_{c}=\Psi_{c,1}\land\Psi_{c,2}
appearing in some formula:

\texttt{conjunction(}\Phi_{c}\texttt{,}\Psi_{c,1}\texttt{,}\Psi_{c,2}\texttt{).}

(AF3)

For every disjunction
\Phi_{d}=\Psi_{d,1}\lor\Psi_{d,2}
appearing in some formula:

\texttt{disjunction(}\Phi_{d}\texttt{,}\Psi_{d,1}\texttt{,}\Psi_{d,2}\texttt{).}

(AF4)

For each negation
\Phi_{n}=\lnot\Psi_{n}
appearing in some formula:

\texttt{negation(}\Phi_{n}\texttt{,}\Psi_{n}\texttt{).}

(AF5)

For the static part, we define:

\texttt{tv(}t\texttt{;}f\texttt{).}

(AF6)

atomOcc(A,L) :-

formulaIsAtomOcc(_,A,L).

(AF7)

atom(A) :-

atomOcc(A,_).

(AF8)

1{truthValue(A,T) : tv(T)}1 :-

atom(A).

(AF9)

truthValue(F,
t
) :-

conjunction(F,G,H),

truthValue(G,
t
),

truthValue(H,
t
).

(AF10)

truthValue(F,
f
) :-

conjunction(F,_,_),

not truthValue(F,
t
).

(AF11)

truthValue(F,
f
) :-

disjunction(F,G,H),

truthValue(G,
f
),

truthValue(H,
f
).

(AF12)

truthValue(F,
t
) :-

disjunction(F,_,_),

not truthValue(F,
f
).

(AF13)

truthValue(F,
t
) :-

negation(F,G),

truthValue(G,
f
).

(AF14)

truthValue(F,
f
) :-

negation(F,G),

truthValue(G,
t
).

(AF15)

{atomOccForgotten(A,L)} :-

atomOcc(A,L).

(AF16)

truthValue(F,T) :-

formulaIsAtomOcc(F,A,L),

truthValue(A,T),

not atomOccForgotten(A,L).

(AF17)

truthValue(F,
t
) :-

formulaIsAtomOcc(F,A,L),

truthValue(A,
f
),

atomOccForgotten(A,L).

(AF18)

truthValue(F,
f
) :-

formulaIsAtomOcc(F,A,L),

truthValue(A,
t
),

atomOccForgotten(A,L).

(AF19)

:-

truthValue(F,
f
),

kbMember(F).

(AF20)

#minimize{1,A,L : atomOccForgotten(A,L)}
.

(AF21)

Let
\mathcal{K}
be an arbitrary knowledge base and
P_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathcal{K})
the extended logic program consisting of rules (AF1)–(AF21) included Encoding
8
. Let
P_{\mathrm{f}}^{\prime}(\mathcal{K})
denote the extended logic program
P_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathcal{K})

without
the minimize statement (AF21).

For an answer set
M
we define:

\displaystyle F_{M}

\displaystyle=\{X^{l}\mid X\in\mathsf{At}(\mathcal{K}),\texttt{atomOccForgotten(}X\texttt{,}l\texttt{)}\in M\}

Lemma 11
.

If
M
is an answer set of
P_{\mathrm{f}}^{\prime}(\mathcal{K})
then
(\bigwedge\mathcal{K})[X^{1}\xrightarrow{\text{f}}\top,\bot;\ldots;X^{n}\xrightarrow{\text{f}}\top,\bot]
is consistent with
F_{M}=\{X^{1},\ldots,X^{n}\}
.

Proof.

The rules given in (AF10)–(AF15) model classical entailment wrt.
\land
,
\lor
, and
\lnot
.
To be specific, the rules (AF10) and (AF11) model that the conjunction of two subformulas is only true if both conjuncts are true, and false if it is not true.
In the same fashion, a disjunction is only false if both of its individual disjuncts are false (modeled by (AF12)).
It is true, if it is not false (modeled by (AF13)).
Classical negation, i. e.,
\lnot\Phi
is true if
\Phi
is false, and vice versa, is represented by (AF14) and (AF15).

Furthermore, rule (AF9) represents that each atom
X
is evaluated to either
t
or
f
.
In other words, since we represent that each atom
X\in\mathsf{At}(\mathcal{K})
is assigned a unique truth value, we automatically represent an
interpretation
on
\mathsf{At}(\mathcal{K})
.

Each atom occurrence
X
with label
l
(i, e.,
X^{l}
) is represented as
\texttt{atomOcc(}X,l\texttt{)}
, due to (AF2) and (AF7).
Further, rule (AF16) models that each
X^{l}
could be forgotten by guessing a subset of atom occurrences by means of a cardinality constraint.
More precisely, (AF16) assigns a subset of all atom occurrences
X^{l}
as
\texttt{atomOccForgotten(}X,l\texttt{)}
.
Thus, (AF16) determines
F_{M}
.
For each atom occurrence in this set, the truth value of the subformula containing the atom occurrence is reversed by (AF18) and (AF19), respectively.
Given an interpretation
\omega
, this results in setting an atom occurrence
X^{l}
with
\omega(X)=f
to
t
(AF18), and vice versa (AF19).
If an atom occurrence
X^{l}
is not in
F_{M}
(i. e., if an atom is not forgotten), (AF17) is triggered, and
X^{l}
must conform to
\omega
.

Finally, all formulas in the given knowledge base must be true.
This is ensured by the integrity constraint (AF20) which forbids elements
A\in\mathcal{K}
to be evaluated to
f
.
This also implies that each
X^{l}\in F_{M}
must be assigned the opposite truth value of
X
, which is equivalent to replacing it with
\top
or
\bot
, corresponding on the context.
Thus, since all formulas
A\in\mathcal{K}
must be true, the version of
\mathcal{K}
modified by the forgetting operation, i. e.,
(\bigwedge\mathcal{K})[X^{1}\xrightarrow{\text{f}}\top,\bot;\ldots;X^{n}\xrightarrow{\text{f}}\top,\bot]
, must be consistent.
∎

Theorem
8
.

Let
M_{o}
be an optimal answer set of
P_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathcal{K})
.
Then
|F_{M_{o}}|=\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathcal{K})
.

Proof.

From Lemma
11
it follows that the answer sets of
P_{\mathrm{f}}^{\prime}(\mathcal{K})
correspond exactly to
(\bigwedge\mathcal{K})[X^{1}\xrightarrow{\text{f}}\top,\bot;\ldots;X^{n}\xrightarrow{\text{f}}\top,\bot]
being consistent with
F_{M}=\{X^{1},\ldots,X^{n}\}
.
The minimize statement (AF21) included in
P_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathcal{K})
then ensures that only a minimal number of instances of
atomOccForgotten(
X
,
l
)
are included in the optimal answer set.
Overall, this corresponds to the minimal number of atom occurrences in
\mathcal{K}
being forgotten, which is exactly the definition of
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathcal{K})
.
∎

A.2.3
The Hitting Set Inconsistency Measure

Encoding 9:
Overview of the ASP encoding
P_{\mathrm{h}}(\mathcal{K})
of the hitting set inconsistency measure
\mathcal{I}{{}_{\mathrm{h}}}
.

\mathcal{K}
is the given knowledge base.

For every
A\in\mathcal{K}
:

kbMember(
A
).

(AH1)

For every
X\in\mathsf{At}(\mathcal{K})
:

atom(
X
).

(AH2)

Define
|\mathcal{K}|
interpretations:

interpretation(1..
|\mathcal{K}|
).

(AH3)

For every conjunction
\Phi_{c}=\Psi_{c,1}\land\Psi_{c,2}
appearing in some formula:

\texttt{conjunction(}\Phi_{c}\texttt{,}\Psi_{c,1}\texttt{,}\Psi_{c,2}\texttt{).}

(AH4)

For every disjunction
\Phi_{d}=\Psi_{d,1}\lor\Psi_{d,2}
appearing in some formula:

\texttt{disjunction(}\Phi_{d}\texttt{,}\Psi_{d,1}\texttt{,}\Psi_{d,2}\texttt{).}

(AH5)

For each negation
\Phi_{n}=\lnot\Psi_{n}
appearing in some formula:

\texttt{negation(}\Phi_{n}\texttt{,}\Psi_{n}\texttt{).}

(AH6)

For every formula
\Phi_{a}
which consists of a single atom
X
:

\texttt{formulaIsAtom(}\Phi_{a}\texttt{,}X\texttt{).}

(AH7)

At least one, and at most
|\mathcal{K}|
interpretations must be included in the hitting set:

1{interpretationActive(I) : interpretation(I)}
|\mathcal{K}|
.

(AH8)

For the static part, we define:

\texttt{tv(}t\texttt{;}f\texttt{).}

(AH9)

1{truthValueInt(A,I,T) : tv(T)}1 :-

atom(A),

interpretation(I).

(AH10)

truthValueInt(F,I,
t
) :-

conjunction(F,G,H),

interpretation(I),

truthValueInt(G,I,
t
),

truthValueInt(H,I,
t
).

(AH11)

truthValueInt(F,I,
f
) :-

conjunction(F,_,_),

interpretation(I),

not truthValueInt(F,I,
t
).

(AH12)

truthValueInt(F,I,
f
) :-

disjunction(F,G,H),

interpretation(I),

truthValueInt(G,I,
f
),

truthValueInt(H,I,
f
).

(AH13)

truthValueInt(F,I,
t
) :-

disjunction(F,_,_),

interpretation(I),

not truthValueInt(F,I,
f
).

(AH14)

truthValueInt(F,I,
t
) :-

negation(F,G),

truthValueInt(G,I,
f
).

(AH15)

truthValueInt(F,I,
f
) :-

negation(F,G),

truthValueInt(G,I,
t
).

(AH16)

truthValueInt(F,I,T) :-

formulaIsAtom(F,G),

truthValueInt(G,I,T),

interpretation(I),

tv(T).

(AH17)

truthValue(F,
t
) :-

truthValueInt(F,I,
t
),

kbMember(F),

interpretation(I),

interpretationActive(I).

(AH18)

truthValue(F,
f
) :-

kbMember(F),

not truthValue(F,
t
).

(AH19)

:-

interpretationActive(I),

1 < I,

not interpretationActive(I-1).

(AH20)

:-

truthValue(F,
f
),

kbMember(F).

(AH21)

#minimize{1,I : interpretationActive(I)}
.

(AH22)

Let
\mathcal{K}
be an arbitrary knowledge base and
P_{\mathrm{h}}(\mathcal{K})
the extended logic program consisting of rules (AH1)–(AH22) listed in Encoding
9
in Section
4.4
.

Lemma 12
.

P_{\mathrm{h}}(\mathcal{K})
does not have an answer set if and only if
\mathcal{K}
contains one or more contradictory formulas.

Proof.

Let some
A^{\bot}\in\mathcal{K}
be contradictory.
To begin with, the formulas
A\in\mathcal{K}
and the atoms
X\in\mathsf{At}(\mathcal{K})
are represented in ASP via (AH1) and (AH2).
Moreover, the operators of each (sub-)formula in
\mathcal{K}
is encoded using (AH4)–(AH7).
Thus,
P_{\mathrm{h}}^{\prime}(\mathcal{K})
contains an ASP representation of
\mathcal{K}
itself.
Additionally, (AH3) adds
|\mathcal{K}|
interpretation constants to
P_{\mathrm{h}}^{\prime}(\mathcal{K})
.
By means of (AH10), we assign a distinct truth value (defined by (AH9)) to each
atom/1
for each
interpretation/1
.
(AH11)–(AH16) model classical entailment (analogous to (AF11)–(AF16), but with an additional reference to an interpretation).
Besides, (AH17) models that a formula consisting of an individual atom gets the same truth value as the atom.
Since every atom representation in ASP is connected to a truth value, and the evaluation of the (sub-)formulas is accomplished via (AH11)–(AH17), every formula
A\in\mathcal{K}
(represented by
kbMember/1
) evaluates to either
t
or
f
.
Since we cannot construct an interpretation which satisfies
A^{\bot}
, it would always evaluate to
f
.
However, the integrity constraint (AH21) forbids this, therefore, no answer set can be constructed.
∎

Let
P_{\mathrm{h}}^{\prime}(\mathcal{K})
denote the extended logic program
P_{\mathrm{h}}(\mathcal{K})

without
the minimize statement (AH22).
Let
M
be an answer set of
P_{\mathrm{h}}^{\prime}(\mathcal{K})
.
Further, let
\omega^{\mathrm{asp}}_{i}
,
i\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}|\}
, denote the ASP constants representing interpretations, and let
\omega_{i}
denote the corresponding interpretations in classical logic.
We define the set of interpretations
\omega_{i}
represented in
M
as

\Omega(M)=\{\omega_{i}\mid\texttt{interpretationActive(}\omega^{\mathrm{asp}}_{i}\texttt{)}\in M\}.

Lemma 13
.

If
M
is an answer set of
P_{\mathrm{h}}^{\prime}(\mathcal{K})
then
\Omega(M)
is a hitting set of
\mathcal{K}
.

Proof.

\Omega(M)
is a hitting set if and only if every formula
A\in\mathcal{K}
is satisfied by at least one
\omega_{i}\in\Omega(M)
.
We will first show that for any
\omega_{i}\in\Omega(M)
and
A\in\mathcal{K}
,
\omega_{i}\models A
if and only if
\texttt{interpretationActive(}\omega^{\mathrm{asp}}_{i}\texttt{)}\in M
.

The cardinality constraint (AH8) ensures that the answer set includes at least one instance of
\texttt{interpretationActive(}\omega^{\mathrm{asp}}_{i}\texttt{)}
.
According to (AH8),
interpretation(
\omega_{i}
)
must be included in
M
as well, however, this is always the case due to (AH3).
Moreover,
interpretationActive(
\omega^{\mathrm{asp}}_{i}
)
must be included in
M
in order for (AH19) to be satisfied.
Further, for each
A\in\mathcal{K}
there must be at least one interpretation such that
\texttt{truthValue(}A\texttt{,}t\texttt{)}\in M
, because otherwise,
\texttt{truthValue(}A\texttt{,}f\texttt{)}\in M
(due to (AH19)), and this is forbidden by the integrity constraint (AH21).

As mentioned before, rules (AH11)–(AH17) represent entailment in classical propositional logic wrt. a given interpretation.
Finally, an atom
X\in\mathcal{K}
can only be either true or false, but not both, under a given interpretation, as stated in (AH14).

Consequently, for each formula
A\in\mathcal{K}
there exists at least one interpretation
\omega_{i}
such that
\texttt{truthValueInt(}A\texttt{,}\omega^{\mathrm{asp}}_{i}\texttt{,}t\texttt{)}\in M
.
It follows that for each
A\in\mathcal{K}
, there is also at least one
\texttt{interpretationActive(}\omega^{\mathrm{asp}}_{i}\texttt{)}\in M
.
Hence, for each formula
A\in\mathcal{K}
there exists an
\omega_{i}\in\Omega(M)
such that
\omega_{i}\models A
.
Thus,
\Omega(M)
is a hitting set of
\mathcal{K}
.
∎

Theorem
9
.

Let
M_{o}
be an optimal answer set of
P_{\mathrm{h}}(\mathcal{K})
.
Then
|\Omega(M_{o})|-1=\mathcal{I}{{}_{\mathrm{h}}}(\mathcal{K})
.
If no answer set of
P_{\mathrm{h}}(\mathcal{K})
exists,
\mathcal{I}{{}_{\mathrm{h}}}(\mathcal{K})=\infty
.

Proof.

Lemma
12
shows that
P_{\mathrm{h}}(\mathcal{K})
does not have an answer set if
\mathcal{K}
contains at least one contradictory formula, i. e., if
\mathcal{I}{{}_{\mathrm{h}}}(\mathcal{K})=\infty
.

Further, in the above proof of Lemma
13
we showed that
\Omega(M)
is a hitting set of
\mathcal{K}
wrt. some answer set
M
.
The cardinality of
\Omega(M)
is exactly the number of instances
\texttt{interpretationActive(}\omega^{\mathrm{asp}}_{i}\texttt{)}
, with
i\in\{1,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}|\}
, that are included in
M
.
The minimize statement (AH22) selects the minimal number of such instances.
This represents the selection of the smallest possible hitting set wrt.
\mathcal{K}
.
At last, subtracting
1
from
|\Omega(M_{o})|
corresponds exactly to the definition of
\mathcal{I}{{}_{\mathrm{h}}}(\mathcal{K})
.
∎

A.2.4
The Max-Distance Inconsistency Measure

Encoding 10:
Overview of the ASP encoding
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\max}(\mathcal{K})
of the max-distance inconsistency measure
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{d}}^{\max}
.

\mathcal{K}
is the given knowledge base.

For every
A_{i}\in\mathcal{K}
with
i\in\{0,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}|-1\}
:

kbMember(
A
,
i
).

(ADM1)

For every
X\in\mathsf{At}(\mathcal{K})
:

atom(
X
).

(ADM2)

Define
|\mathcal{K}|+1
interpretations:

interpretation(0..
|\mathcal{K}|
).

(ADM3)

For every conjunction
\Phi_{c}=\Psi_{c,1}\land\Psi_{c,2}
appearing in some formula:

\texttt{conjunction(}\Phi_{c}\texttt{,}\Psi_{c,1}\texttt{,}\Psi_{c,2}\texttt{).}

(ADM4)

For every disjunction
\Phi_{d}=\Psi_{d,1}\lor\Psi_{d,2}
appearing in some formula:

\texttt{disjunction(}\Phi_{d}\texttt{,}\Psi_{d,1}\texttt{,}\Psi_{d,2}\texttt{).}

(ADM5)

For each negation
\Phi_{n}=\lnot\Psi_{n}
appearing in some formula:

\texttt{negation(}\Phi_{n}\texttt{,}\Psi_{n}\texttt{).}

(ADM6)

For every formula
\Phi_{a}
which consists of a single atom
X
:

\texttt{formulaIsAtom(}\Phi_{a}\texttt{,}X\texttt{).}

(ADM7)

Distance between interpretation
|\mathcal{K}|
and the models of each formula:

d(X) :-

diff(X),

interpretation(I),

X <= #count{A: atom(A),

truthValueInt(A,I,T),

not truthValueInt(A,
|\mathcal{K}|
,T)}.

(ADM8)

For the static part, we define:

\texttt{tv(}t\texttt{;}f\texttt{).}

(ADM9)

1{truthValueInt(A,I,T) : tv(T)}1 :-

atom(A),

interpretation(I).

(ADM10)

truthValueInt(F,I,
t
) :-

conjunction(F,G,H),

interpretation(I),

truthValueInt(G,I,
t
),

truthValueInt(H,I,
t
).

(ADM11)

truthValueInt(F,I,
f
) :-

conjunction(F,_,_),

interpretation(I),

not truthValueInt(F,I,
t
).

(ADM12)

truthValueInt(F,I,
f
) :-

disjunction(F,G,H),

interpretation(I),

truthValueInt(G,I,
f
),

truthValueInt(H,I,
f
).

(ADM13)

truthValueInt(F,I,
t
) :-

disjunction(F,_,_),

interpretation(I),

not truthValueInt(F,I,
f
).

(ADM14)

truthValueInt(F,I,
t
) :-

negation(F,G),

truthValueInt(G,I,
f
).

(ADM15)

truthValueInt(F,I,
f
) :-

negation(F,G),

truthValueInt(G,I,
t
).

(ADM16)

truthValueInt(F,I,T) :-

formulaIsAtom(F,G),

truthValueInt(G,I,T),

interpretation(I),

tv(T).

(ADM17)

:-

truthValueInt(F,I,
f
),

kbMember(F,I).

(ADM18)

diff(1..X) :-

X = #count{A: atom(A)}.

(ADM19)

#minimize{1,X : d(X)}.

(ADM20)

Let
\mathcal{K}
be an arbitrary knowledge base and
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\max}(\mathcal{K})
the extended logic program consisting of rules (ADM1)–(ADM22) listed in Encoding
10
in Section
4.5
.

Lemma 14
.

P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\max}(\mathcal{K})
does not have an answer set if and only if
\mathcal{K}
contains one or more contradictory formulas.

Proof.

The knowledge base, i. e., its formulas, subformulas, and atoms are modelled by (ADM1)–(ADM2) and (ADM4)–(ADM7).
Note that each
A\in\mathcal{K}
gets a label
i
, and is represented as
\texttt{kbMember(}A,i\texttt{)}
(ADM1).
The two truth values are represented by (ADM9).
Moreover, via (ADM3) we represent
|\mathcal{K}|+1
interpretations in
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\max}(\mathcal{K})
.
We denote these interpretations as follows (note that we represent
\omega_{i}
simply as
i
in ASP):

\displaystyle\omega_{i}(X)=\begin{cases}t&\quad\texttt{truthValue(}X,i,t\texttt{)}\in M\\
f&\quad\texttt{truthValue(}X,i,f\texttt{)}\in M\end{cases}

Analogously to (AH10) (wrt.
\mathcal{I}{{}_{\mathrm{h}}}
), (ADM10) guesses all
|\mathcal{K}|+1
interpretations by assigning each atom in each interpretation a distinct truth value.
(ADM11)–(ADM17) correspond exactly to (AH11)–(AH17) (wrt.
\mathcal{I}{{}_{\mathrm{h}}}
).
Thus, these rules model classical entailment.

Each interpretation
\omega_{i}
is supposed to be a model for formula
A_{i}\in\mathcal{K}
,
i\in\{0,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}|-1\}
.
To this end, the integrity constraint (ADM18) ensures that no formula
A_{i}
evaluates to
f
under
\omega_{i}
.
If a contradictory formula
A^{\bot}_{i}
is included in
\mathcal{K}
, there exists no interpretation
\omega_{i}
that would satisfy
A^{\bot}_{i}
.
Hence, the answer set would need to include
\texttt{truthValueInt(}A^{\bot},i,f\texttt{)}
.
However, (ADM18) does not allow this, so no answer set can be derived.
∎

Let
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\max}{{}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{K})
denote the extended logic program
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\max}(\mathcal{K})

without
the minimize statement (ADM20).
Let
D^{\max}_{M}=\{n\mid\texttt{d(}n\texttt{)}\in M,n\in\mathbb{N}\}
wrt. an answer set
M
.

Lemma 15
.

Let
M
be an answer set of
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\max}{{}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{K})
.
Each
n\in D^{\max}_{M}
corresponds to a value smaller than or equal to the distance between some interpretation
\omega_{|\mathcal{K}|}
and one of the models of the formulas in
\mathcal{K}
, i. e.,
\omega_{i}
with
i\in\{0,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}|-1\}
.

Proof.

If an atom
X\in\mathsf{At}(\mathcal{K})
evaluates to a truth value
\theta\in\{t,f\}
under an interpretation
\omega_{i}
, and not to truth value
\theta
under interpretation
\omega_{|\mathcal{K}|}
, with
i\in\{0,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}|-1\}
, then
\texttt{truthValueInt(}X,i,\theta\texttt{)}\in M
and
\texttt{truthValueInt(}X,|\mathcal{K}|,\theta\texttt{)}\notin M
.
We also have
\texttt{atom(}X\texttt{)}\in M
,
\texttt{interpretation(}i\texttt{)}\in M
, and
\texttt{interpretation(}|\mathcal{K}|\texttt{)}\in M
.
Consequently, if for
n
cases of atoms
X
wrt. interpretations
\omega_{i}
and
\omega_{|\mathcal{K}|}
we have different truth values, i. e., we have
\texttt{truthValueInt(}X,i,\theta\texttt{)}\in M
, but
\texttt{truthValueInt(}X,|\mathcal{K}|,\theta\texttt{)}\notin M
, this is counted by means of the
#count
aggregate in (ADM8).
Note that this also represents the Dalal distance between
\omega_{i}
and
\omega_{|\mathcal{K}|}
.
Moreover,
\{\texttt{diff(0),}\ldots,\texttt{diff(}|\mathsf{At}(\mathcal{K})|\texttt{)}\}\subset M
(ADM19).
Since the Dalal distance between
\omega_{i}
and
\omega_{|\mathcal{K}|}
cannot exceed
|\mathsf{At}(\mathcal{K})|
(except for the
\infty
case, see Lemma
14
),
\texttt{diff(}n\texttt{)}\in M
.
Moreover, via (ADM8), we do not only derive
\texttt{d(}n\texttt{)}
, but also
\texttt{d(}m\texttt{)}
with
m\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
.
Thus, we conclude that
\{\texttt{d(1)},\ldots,\texttt{d(}n\texttt{)}\}\subset M
if the Dalal distance between
\omega_{i}
and
\omega_{|\mathcal{K}|}
is
n
.
Further, each interpretation
\omega_{i}
represents a model of the
i
-th formula, due to the integrity constraint (ADM18).
Hence, each element in
D^{\max}_{M}
always represents a value smaller than or equal to the Dalal distance between a model of a formula in
\mathcal{K}
and
\omega_{|\mathcal{K}|}
.
∎

Theorem
10
.

Let
M_{o}
be an optimal answer set of
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\max}(\mathcal{K})
.
Then
|D^{\max}_{M_{o}}|=\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{d}}^{\max}(\mathcal{K})
.
If no answer set of
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\max}(\mathcal{K})
exists,
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{d}}^{\max}(\mathcal{K})=\infty
.

Proof.

Lemma
14
shows that
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\max}(\mathcal{K})
does not have an answer set if
\mathcal{K}
contains at least one contradictory formula, i. e., if
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{d}}^{\max}(\mathcal{K})=\infty
.

Furthermore, Lemma
15
shows that the elements in
D^{\max}_{M}
represent the Dalal distances between an interpretation
\omega_{|\mathcal{K}|}
and a model of one of the formulas, represented by
\omega_{i}
,
i\in\{0,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}|-1\}
.
Let
M_{o}
be an optimal answer set of
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\max}(\mathcal{K})
.
The minimize statement (ADM20) finds the interpretation (
\omega_{|\mathcal{K}|}
) with the “optimal” distance to the models of the formulas of
\mathcal{K}
.
More precisely, the objective of the minimize statement is to find as few different distance values as possible, since it “counts” each distance.
Note that each distinct distance can only be derived once (due to (ADM19)), and for every
\texttt{d(}n\texttt{)}\in M
with
n\in D^{\max}_{M_{o}}
, we also have
\texttt{d(}m\texttt{)}\in M
with
m\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
.
In other words, every distance that is smaller than
n
(but at least 1, since
\texttt{diff(0)}\notin M
(ADM19)) is also counted, since (ADM8) derives
\texttt{d(}n\texttt{)}
for each value that is smaller than or equal to the Dalal distance (see Lemma
15
).
Hence, we have
D^{\max}_{M_{o}}=\{1,\ldots,n_{\max}\}
with
n_{max}
corresponding to the maximal value in
D^{\max}_{M_{o}}
.
Due to the minimization in (ADM20),
n_{\max}
corresponds exactly to the minimal maximum distance.
It is easy to see that
|D^{\max}_{M_{o}}|=n_{\max}
.
Therefore,
|D^{\max}_{M_{o}}|=\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{d}}^{\max}(\mathcal{K})
.
∎

(ADS18)

Let
\mathcal{K}
be an arbitrary knowledge base and
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{K})
the extended logic program consisting of rules (ADS1)–(ADS22) listed in Encoding
11
in Section

.

Lemma 16
.

P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{K})
does not have an answer set if and only if

contains one or more contradictory formulas.

Proof.

Analogous to the proof of Lemma
14
.
∎

We define

D^{\Sigma}_{M}=\{\mathrm{diff}_{M}(X,i)\mid\texttt{truthValueInt(}X,i,\theta\texttt{)}\in M,\texttt{truthValueInt(}X,|\mathcal{K}|,\theta\texttt{)}\notin M\}.

Theorem
11
.

Let
M_{o}
be an optimal answer set of
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{K})
.
Then
|D^{\Sigma}_{M_{o}}|=\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{d}}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{K})
.
If no answer set of
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{K})
exists,
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{d}}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{K})=\infty
.

Proof.

Lemma
16
shows that
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{K})
does not have an answer set if
\mathcal{K}
contains at least one contradictory formula, i. e., if
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{d}}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{K})=\infty
.

Each formula
A\in\mathcal{K}
is connected to one specific interpretation.
More precisely, interpretation
\omega_{i}
is a model of formula
A_{i}
(ADS18) (see also the proof for Lemma
15
).
Further, the inner part of the minimize statement (ADS8) represents the calculation and summation of the Dalal distance between
\omega_{|\mathcal{K}|}
and each interpretation
\omega_{i}
with
i\in\{0,\ldots,|\mathcal{K}|-1\}
.
To be precise, each case in which an atom
X
evaluates to
\theta\in\{t,f\}
under interpretation
\omega_{i}
, but
\omega_{|\mathcal{K}|}\neq\theta
, i. e., if
\texttt{truthValue(}X,i,\theta\texttt{)}\in M
, but
\texttt{truthValue(}X,|\mathcal{K}|,\theta\texttt{)}\notin M
, is counted as
1
.
Thus, for any answer set
M
,
|D^{\Sigma}_{M}|
corresponds to the sum of Dalal distances between the models of the formulas
\omega_{i}
and
\omega_{|\mathcal{K}|}
.
For an optimal answer set
M_{o}
, yielded by the
\#minimize
statement in (ADS8),
\omega_{|\mathcal{K}|}
becomes the “optimal” interpretation, i. e., the one with the lowest sum of distances.
Hence,
|D^{\Sigma}_{M_{o}}|=\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{d}}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{K})
.
∎

A.2.6
The Hit-Distance Inconsistency Measure

Encoding 12:
Overview of the ASP encoding
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\mathrm{hit}}(\mathcal{K})
of the hit-distance inconsistency measure
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{d}}^{\text{hit}}
.

\mathcal{K}
is the given knowledge base.

For every
A\in\mathcal{K}
:

kbMember(
A
).

(ADH1)

For every
X\in\mathsf{At}(\mathcal{K})
:

atom(
X
).

(ADH2)

For every conjunction
\Phi_{c}=\Psi_{c,1}\land\Psi_{c,2}
appearing in some formula:

\texttt{conjunction(}\Phi_{c}\texttt{,}\Psi_{c,1}\texttt{,}\Psi_{c,2}\texttt{).}

(ADH3)

For every disjunction
\Phi_{d}=\Psi_{d,1}\lor\Psi_{d,2}
appearing in some formula:

\texttt{disjunction(}\Phi_{d}\texttt{,}\Psi_{d,1}\texttt{,}\Psi_{d,2}\texttt{).}

(ADH4)

For each negation
\Phi_{n}=\lnot\Psi_{n}
appearing in some formula:

\texttt{negation(}\Phi_{n}\texttt{,}\Psi_{n}\texttt{).}

(ADH5)

For every formula
\Phi_{a}
which consists of a single atom
X
:

\texttt{formulaIsAtom(}\Phi_{a}\texttt{,}X\texttt{).}

(ADH6)

For the static part, we define:

\texttt{tv(}t\texttt{;}f\texttt{).}

(ADH7)

1{truthValue(A,T) : tv(T)}1 :-

atom(A).

(ADH8)

truthValue(F,
t
) :-

conjunction(F,G,H),

truthValue(G,
t
),

truthValue(H,
t
).

(ADH9)

truthValue(F,
f
) :-

conjunction(F,_,_),

not truthValue(F,
t
).

(ADH10)

truthValue(F,
f
) :-

disjunction(F,G,H),

truthValue(G,
f
),

truthValue(H,
f
).

(ADH11)

truthValue(F,
t
) :-

disjunction(F,_,_),

not truthValue(F,
f
).

(ADH12)

truthValue(F,
t
) :-

negation(F,G),

truthValue(G,
f
).

(ADH13)

truthValue(F,
f
) :-

negation(F,G),

truthValue(G,
t
).

(ADH14)

truthValue(F,T) :-

formulaIsAtom(F,G),

truthValue(G,T),

tv(T).

(ADH15)

truthValueKbMember(F,T) :-

kbMember(F),

tv(T),

truthValue(F,T).

(ADH16)

#minimize{1,F : truthValueKbMember(F,
f
)}.

(ADH17)

Let
\mathcal{K}
be an arbitrary knowledge base and
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\mathrm{hit}}(\mathcal{K})
the extended logic program consisting of rules (ADH1)–(ADH17) listed in Encoding
12
in Section
4.7
.
Let
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\mathrm{hit}}{{}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{K})
denote the extended logic program
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\mathrm{hit}}(\mathcal{K})
without the minimize statement (ADH17).

For an answer set
M
of
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\mathrm{hit}}{{}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{K})
we define:

K_{M}=\{A^{\mathrm{asp}}\mid A\in\mathcal{K},\texttt{truthValueKbMember(}A^{\mathrm{asp}},f\texttt{)}\in M\}

with
A^{\mathrm{asp}}
being an ASP representation of
A
.

Lemma 17
.

Each
A^{\mathrm{asp}}\in K_{M}
corresponds to a formula
A\in\mathcal{K}
being evaluated to
f
.

Proof.

The knowledge base, i. e., its formulas, subformulas, and atoms are modeled by (ADH1)–(ADH6).
(This corresponds exactly to (AC1)–(AC6) wrt.
\mathcal{I}{{}_{\mathrm{c}}}
.)
The two truth values are represented by (ADH7).
Analogously to (AF10) (
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{f}}
), (ADH8) guesses an interpretation by assigning each atom a distinct truth value.
We denote this interpretation as follows:

\displaystyle\omega_{M}(X)=\begin{cases}t&\quad\texttt{truthValue(}X\texttt{,}t\texttt{)}\in M\\
f&\quad\texttt{truthValue(}X\texttt{,}f\texttt{)}\in M\end{cases}

(ADH9)–(ADH14) model the evaluation of conjunctions, disjunctions, and negations wrt.
\omega_{M}
(this corresponds exactly to (AF11)–(AF16) wrt.
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{f}}
).
In addition, (ADH15) models that a formula consisting of an individual atom gets the same truth value as that atom.
Moreover, (ADH16) essentially extracts the truth values of the formulas
A\in\mathcal{K}
.
More precisely, if the atom valuations determined by (ADH8) eventually lead to a formula being evaluated to a truth value
\theta\in\{t,f\}
—i. e., if
\texttt{truthValue(}A^{\mathrm{asp}},\theta\texttt{)}\in M
and
A
is an element of
\mathcal{K}
(i. e.,
\texttt{kbMember(}A^{\mathrm{asp}}\texttt{)}\in M
), then
\texttt{truthValueKbMember(}A^{\mathrm{asp}},\theta\texttt{)}\in M
.
Hence, each
A^{\mathrm{asp}}\in K_{M}
corresponds to a formula
A\in\mathcal{K}
with
\omega_{M}(A)=f
.
∎

Theorem
12
.

Let
M_{o}
be an optimal answer set of
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\mathrm{hit}}(\mathcal{K})
.
Then
|K_{M_{o}}|=\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{d}}^{\text{hit}}(\mathcal{K})
.

Proof.

From Lemma
17
we know that for an answer set
M
of
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\mathrm{hit}}{{}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{K})
, each
A^{\mathrm{asp}}\in K_{M}
corresponds to a formula
A\in\mathcal{K}
being evaluated to false (i. e., with
\omega_{M}(A)=f
).
In
P_{\mathrm{d}}^{\mathrm{hit}}(\mathcal{K})
, the minimization statement (ADH17) ensures that the resulting optimal answer set
M_{o}
contains a minimal number of instances of
\texttt{truthvalueKbMember(}A^{\mathrm{asp}},f\texttt{)}
(with
A\in\mathcal{K}
), i. e., the cardinality of
|K_{M_{o}}|
gets minimized.
Hence, we are computing the minimal number of formulas in
\mathcal{K}
which evaluate to
f
(and which would need to be removed in order to render
\mathcal{K}
consistent), which meets the definition of
\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{d}}^{\text{hit}}
.
∎

B.1
Runtime Measurements

The following figures display cactus plots
24
24

24

A cactus plot is comprised of time measurements ordered from low to high.
Hence, we measure the runtime of an algorithm wrt. each knowledge base of a given data set, and subsequently order the measurements from low to high and plot them.
If an inconsistency value wrt. a given knowledge base could not be computed within the given time limit (i.e., if it timed out) or if a memory error occurred, the corresponding time measurement is not plotted.
, each corresponding to a comparison between the three approaches wrt. a specific inconsistency measure and data set.
